]
.

Out of the seven left coset spaces that can be obtained from (
52
), the following six of them

\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}}),\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}}),\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}}),\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}),\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}}),\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})

are symmetric spaces, as the subgroup is the subgroup of elements fixed by an involution (Theorem II.1.3a of
[
Loo69
]
).

Out of these six symmetric spaces, the following four

\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}}),\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}}),\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}}),\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}})

are hermitian symmetric spaces, since
\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
and
\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
are compact. In the same way that we chose the Kähler structure on
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(V)
in Remark
3.4
, we can construct invariant Kähler structures on
\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
,
\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
,
\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
, from the fact that
\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{ad}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
is a linear complex structure on
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Since
\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
is compact and
\tilde{G}
,
\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})
,
\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
, are noncompact,
\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
is a hermitian symmetric space of compact type, and
\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}}),\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}}),\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}})

are hermitian symmetric spaces of noncompact type. By choosing basepoints and by Theorem VI.1.1.(iii) of
[
Hel01
]
, we have

\displaystyle(\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{\geq 0})^{-1}(W)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}})

\displaystyle\cong\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}

(58)

\displaystyle\varphi^{-1}(\mathbf{F})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}})

\displaystyle\cong\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}

\displaystyle\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}})

\displaystyle\cong\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}

for
W\in\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n};V)
and
\mathbf{F}\in\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n};V)
. In particular,
(\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{\geq 0})^{-1}(W)
and
\varphi^{-1}(\mathbf{F})
obtain complex structures from the invariant complex structures on
\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}}))
and
\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
. By the Harish-Chandra embedding, these spaces are biholomorphic to bounded symmetric domains.

4
Factorizations of orbit fibrations

In this section, we construct the fiber bundles of interest
\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}
,
\beta_{\mathbf{J}}
,
\eta_{\mathbf{J}}
as a factor of the fiber bundles of simultaneous linear symplectic reduction, first in the reduced case, and then in the nonreduced case. We follow the proof of Theorem 2 of
[
Tak68
]
. We also discuss how the symplectic twistor Grassmannians are as fiber products of
\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}
and
\beta_{\mathbf{J}}
, with a corrected diffeomorphism.

4.1
Fibers of
\alpha
,
\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{0}
, and
(\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega})
as vector bundles

Since
\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})
and
\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
are noncompact, the two symmetric spaces
\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})
,
\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
do not admit
\tilde{G}
-invariant Riemannian metrics (Theorem IV.2.5b of
[
Hel01
]
). Identifying by choosing appropriate basepoints we get

\displaystyle(\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega})^{-1}(W_{0})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\operatorname{Gr}(0,n_{+},n_{-};\tilde{W})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})

(59)

\displaystyle(\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{0})^{-1}(W_{0})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(0,n_{+}.n_{-};\tilde{W})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}}),

and applying Theorem IV.3.5 of
[
Loo69
]
, we obtain diffeomorphisms to total spaces of vector bundles:

\displaystyle(\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega})^{-1}(W_{0})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\tilde{K}\times_{\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}

(60)

\displaystyle(\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{0})^{-1}(W_{0})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\tilde{K}\times_{\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}.

For the left coset space,
\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}})

we show an analogous diffeomorphism directly, and reshow it for the symmetric spaces
\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})
and
\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
. From the Cartan decomposition of Lie groups

\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})\cong\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}\times\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel},\quad\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})\cong\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}\times\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times},

we use the fact that every
\tilde{g}\in\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})
can be expressed uniquely as
\tilde{g}=\tilde{k}^{\shortparallel}\exp\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}
for some
\tilde{k}^{\shortparallel}\in\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}
and
\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}\in\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}
, and every
\tilde{g}\in\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
can be expressed uniquely as
\tilde{g}=\tilde{k}^{\shortparallel}\exp\tilde{p}^{\times}
for some
\tilde{k}^{\shortparallel}\in\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}
and
\tilde{p}^{\times}\in\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Suppose
W_{0}\in\operatorname{Gr}(n_{0},0,n-n_{0};V)
, and
(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})
,
(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\mathbf{F})
,
(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},W^{\prime})
are compatible triples.

1.

There are diffeomorphisms

\displaystyle\tilde{K}\times_{\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\alpha^{-1}(W_{0})

\displaystyle{[(\tilde{k},\tilde{p})]}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\tilde{k}\exp\tilde{p}.\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}

\displaystyle\tilde{K}\times_{\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle(\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{0})^{-1}(W_{0})

\displaystyle{[(\tilde{k},\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}+\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times})]}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\tilde{k}\exp\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}.\mathbf{F}

\displaystyle\tilde{K}\times_{\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle(\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega})^{-1}(W_{0})

\displaystyle{[(\tilde{k},\tilde{p}^{\times}+\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel})]}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\tilde{k}\exp\tilde{p}^{\times}.W^{\prime}.

\tilde{G}
acts on the fiber spaces by
\tilde{G}=\operatorname{Sp}(W_{0}^{\omega}/W_{0})\cong\operatorname{Sp}_{W_{0}}(V)/(\operatorname{GL}(W_{0})\ltimes H(W_{0}))
, and the diffeomorphisms are
\tilde{K}=\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
-equivariant.

2.

The diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}\times_{\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{-1}(W_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}/\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}.\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(61)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}\times_{\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{0})^{-1}(W_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}/\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}.\mathbf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\mathbf{F})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(62)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}\times_{\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega})^{-1}(W_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}/\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{K}.W^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},W^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(63)

commute. Here the upper horizontal arrows are defined from the previous statement, and

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})}(\tilde{k}\exp\tilde{p}.\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\tilde{k}.\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}

(64)

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\mathbf{F})}(\tilde{k}\exp(\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}).\mathbf{F})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\tilde{k}.\mathbf{F}

(65)

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},W^{\prime})}(\tilde{k}\exp(\tilde{p}^{\times}).W^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\tilde{k}.W^{\prime}

(66)

are
\tilde{K}
-equivariant.

3.

The maps (
64
), (
65
), (
66
) depend on the choices involved in defining them as follows. If
g\in\tilde{G}

\displaystyle g^{-1}\cdot\pi_{(\operatorname{Ad}(g)\tilde{I},\operatorname{Ad}(g)\tilde{\mathbf{J}},g.\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})}(g\cdot)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})}(\cdot)

\displaystyle g^{-1}\cdot\pi_{(\operatorname{Ad}(g)\tilde{I},\operatorname{Ad}(g)\tilde{\mathbf{J}},g.\mathbf{F})}(g\cdot)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\mathbf{F})}(\cdot)

\displaystyle g^{-1}\cdot\pi_{(\operatorname{Ad}(g)\tilde{I},\operatorname{Ad}(g)\tilde{\mathbf{J}},g.W^{\prime})}(g\cdot)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},W^{\prime})}(\cdot).

The maps (
64
), (
65
), (
66
) do not depend on the choice of basepoint in each
\tilde{K}
-orbit–i.e. for any
\tilde{k}\in\tilde{K}
,

\displaystyle\pi_{(\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{k})\tilde{I},\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{k})\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\tilde{k}.\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})}

\displaystyle\pi_{(\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{k})\tilde{I},\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{k})\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\tilde{k}.\mathbf{F})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\mathbf{F})}

\displaystyle\pi_{(\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{k})\tilde{I},\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{k})\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\tilde{k}.W^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},W^{\prime})}.

Proof.

(1): The map
\tilde{K}\times\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}\to\tilde{G}
given by
(\tilde{k},\tilde{p})\mapsto\tilde{k}\cdot\exp\tilde{p}
is a diffeomorphism (Theorem VI.1.1 (iii) of
[
Hel01
]
), and descends to a diffeomorphism on
\tilde{K}\times_{\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}\to\tilde{G}/\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}
, as

\tilde{k}\cdot\exp(p)\cdot\tilde{k}^{\shortparallel}=\tilde{k}\tilde{k}^{\shortparallel}\cdot\exp(\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{k}^{\shortparallel})^{-1}\tilde{p})\quad\tilde{k}^{\shortparallel}\in\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel},\tilde{p}\in\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}.

Since
\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}
is identified with the stabilizer of
\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}
in
\operatorname{Sp}_{W}(V)/(\operatorname{GL}(V)\ltimes H(W))
,
\tilde{G}/\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}
is diffeomorphic to
\alpha^{-1}(W)
by the map
g\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}\mapsto g\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}.\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}=g.\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}
.

Consider the map
\tilde{K}\times\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}\times\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}\to\tilde{G}
given by
(\tilde{k},\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel},\tilde{p}^{\times})\mapsto\tilde{k}\cdot\exp\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}\cdot\exp\tilde{p}^{\times}
. This is a diffeomorphism by Theorem IV.3.2 of
[
Loo69
]
, Lemma 6 of
[
Tak68
]
, Lemma 9.10 of
[
Wol72
]
, Lemma 11.6 of
[
Wol69
]
. For any
\tilde{k}_{1}^{\shortparallel}\exp\tilde{p}_{1}^{\times}\in\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
, there exists
\tilde{k}_{2}^{\shortparallel}\exp\tilde{p}_{2}^{\times}\in\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
such that

\displaystyle\tilde{k}\exp\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}\exp\tilde{p}^{\times}\tilde{k}_{1}^{\shortparallel}\exp\tilde{p}_{1}^{\times}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{k}\tilde{k}_{1}^{\shortparallel}\exp(\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{k}_{1}^{\shortparallel})^{-1}\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel})\exp(\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{k}_{1}^{\shortparallel})^{-1}\tilde{p}^{\times})\exp\tilde{p}_{1}^{\times}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{k}\tilde{k}_{1}^{\shortparallel}\exp(\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{k}_{1}^{\shortparallel})^{-1}\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel})\tilde{k}_{2}^{\shortparallel}\exp\tilde{p}_{2}^{\times},

because
\exp(\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{k}_{1}^{\shortparallel})^{-1}\tilde{p}^{\times})\exp\tilde{p}_{1}^{\times}\in\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
, and every element of
\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
can be expressed in the form
\tilde{k}_{2}^{\shortparallel}\exp\tilde{p}_{2}^{\times}
.
So the diffeomorphism
\tilde{K}\times\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}\times\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}\to\tilde{G}
descends to a diffeomorphism

\tilde{K}\times_{\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times})\to\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}}).

Since
(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\mathbf{F})
is a compatible triple,
\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
is the stabilizer of
\mathbf{F}
in
\tilde{G}
, so
\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
is diffeomorphic to
(\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{0})^{-1}
. The diffeomorphism for
(\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega})^{-1}(W)
is proven similarly. (2) is immediate. (3):

Suppose
\tilde{\mathbf{F}}^{\oplus}=\tilde{k}.\exp(p).\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}=\tilde{k}g^{-1}\exp(gpg^{-1}).(g.\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})
. Then

\displaystyle g^{-1}\cdot\pi_{(\operatorname{Ad}(g)\tilde{I},\operatorname{Ad}(g)\tilde{\mathbf{J}},g.\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})}(g.\tilde{\mathbf{F}}^{\oplus})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{-1}\cdot g\tilde{k}g^{-1}.(g.\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I},\tilde{\mathbf{J}},\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})}(\tilde{\mathbf{F}}^{\oplus}).

The group action for (
65
), (
66
) can be verified similarly. The independence property follows by
\tilde{K}
-equivariance of the maps (
64
), (
65
), (
66
).
∎

Remark 4.2
.

If
\tilde{\mathbf{J}}
is descended from an
\omega
-compatible linear complex structure
\mathbf{J}
on
V
, and
\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}\in\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
,
\mathbf{F}\in\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
, or
W^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})
, we can take

\displaystyle\tilde{K}.\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\alpha|_{\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})})^{-1}(W_{0})

\displaystyle\tilde{K}.\mathbf{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{0}|_{\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})})^{-1}(W_{0})

\displaystyle\tilde{K}.W^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle((\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega})|_{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})})^{-1}(W_{0})

in the diagrams (
61
), (
62
), (
63
).

Remark 4.3
.

When
W_{0}
is the zero vector space, the diffeomorphism for
(\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{0})^{-1}(W_{0})\cong\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(0,n_{+},n_{-};V)
of Lemma
4.1
.1 appears in Theorem 2 of
[
Tak68
]
, and the associated bundle in (
62
) is
isomorphic as vector bundles
to the normal bundle of
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbf{J}}(0,n_{+},n_{-};V)
in
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(0,n_{+},n_{-};V)
. Our proof follows the idea in the proof of Theorem 2 of
[
Tak68
]
. Compare with the holomorphic version in Theorem 11.8.2 of
[
Wol69
]
and the second statement of the Orbit Fibration Theorem of
[
Wol72
]
, where
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(0,n_{+},n_{-};V)
is identified with a
relatively compact
tubular neighborhood of the holomorphic normal bundle of
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbf{J}}(0,n_{+},n_{-};V)
in
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(0,n_{+},n_{-};V)
.

4.2
Factorization of orbit fibrations

In this section, we will assume the
\tilde{\omega}
-compatible linear complex structure
\tilde{\mathbf{J}}=\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W)
on
\tilde{W}=W^{\omega}/W
is induced from
\mathbf{J}
as a function of every isotropic subspace
W=W_{0}\subseteq V
.

Proposition 4.4

(Uniqueness of compatible triples)
.

1.

If
W^{\prime}\in(\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega})|_{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})}^{-1}(W_{0})
, then there exists a unique involution

\tilde{I}(W^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Sp}_{W^{\prime}/W_{0}}(W_{0}^{\omega}/W_{0})

such that
(\tilde{I}(W^{\prime}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0}),W^{\prime})
is a compatible triple and
W^{\prime}/W_{0}
is the linear subspace fixed by
\tilde{I}(W^{\prime})
.

2.

If
\mathbf{F}\in(\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{0})|_{\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}^{-1}(W_{0})
, then there exists a unique involution

\tilde{I}(\mathbf{F})\in\operatorname{Sp}_{\mathbf{F}/\mathbf{F}_{0}}(W_{0}^{\omega}/W_{0})

such that
(\tilde{I}(\mathbf{F}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0}),\mathbf{F})
is a compatible triple and
(\mathbf{F}/\mathbf{F}_{0})\cap\mathbf{F}_{\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})}
is the linear subspace fixed by
\tilde{I}(\mathbf{F})|_{\mathbf{F}/\mathbf{F}_{0}}
.

3.

If
\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}\in\alpha|_{\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}(\vec{n})}^{-1}(W_{0})
, then there exists a unique involution

\tilde{I}(\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})\in\operatorname{Sp}_{\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}/\mathbf{F}_{0}}(W_{0}^{\omega}/W_{0})

such that
(\tilde{I}(\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0}),\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})
is a compatible triple and
\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}_{\geq 0}/\mathbf{F}_{0}
is the linear subspace fixed by
\tilde{I}(\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})|_{\mathbf{F}/\mathbf{F}_{0}}
.

Proof.

(1): Consider the splitting

W_{0}^{\omega}/W_{0}=(W^{\prime}/W_{0})\oplus((W^{\prime})^{\omega}/W_{0})

into
\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})
-invariant symplectic subspaces. Then let
\tilde{I}(W^{\prime})(\mathbf{w}+\mathbf{w}^{\prime}):=\mathbf{w}^{\prime}-\mathbf{w}^{\prime\prime}
for
\mathbf{w}\in W^{\prime}/W_{0}
,
\mathbf{w}^{\prime\prime}\in(W^{\prime})^{\omega}/W_{0}
.
\tilde{I}(W^{\prime})
is an involution, its
+1
-eigenspace is
W^{\prime}/W_{0}
, and commutes with
\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})
. If
g\in\operatorname{Sp}_{W^{\prime}/W_{0}}(W_{0}^{\omega}/W_{0})^{\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0}))}
, then since
(W^{\prime})^{\omega}/W_{0}
is the
\tilde{\omega}(\cdot,\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})\cdot)
-orthogonal complement of
W^{\prime}/W_{0}
and
g
preserves orthogonal complements,
g=g|_{W^{\prime}/W_{0}}+g|_{(W^{\prime})^{\omega}/W_{0}}
. Then
g
commutes with
\tilde{I}(W^{\prime})
. Conversely, if
g
commutes with both
\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})
and
\tilde{I}(W^{\prime})
,
g
preserves the eigenspaces of
\tilde{I}(W^{\prime})
and
\tilde{\omega}(\cdot,\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})\cdot)
orthogonal complements, so
g=g|_{W^{\prime}/W_{0}}+g|_{(W^{\prime})^{\omega}/W_{0}}
, so
g\in\operatorname{Sp}_{W^{\prime}/W_{0}}(W_{0}^{\omega}/W_{0})^{\operatorname{Ad}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}(W_{0})})}
. So
(\tilde{I}(W^{\prime}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0}),W^{\prime})
is a compatible triple. If
\tilde{I}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Sp}_{W^{\prime}/W_{0}}(W_{0}^{\omega}/W_{0})
is an involution that commutes
\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})
, and whose
+1
-eigenspace is
W^{\prime}/W_{0}
, then its
-1
-eigenspace has to be equal to the
\tilde{\omega}(\cdot,\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})\cdot)
-orthogonal complement of
W^{\prime}/W_{0}
. So
\tilde{I}^{\prime}=\tilde{I}(W^{\prime})
.

(2):
(W_{0}^{\omega}/W_{0})^{\mathbb{C}}
splits into
\tilde{\omega}^{\mathbb{C}}(\cdot,\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})\overline{\cdot})
-orthogonal subspaces

((\mathbf{F}/\mathbf{F}_{0})\cap\mathbf{F}_{\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})})\oplus(\overline{(\mathbf{F}/\mathbf{F}_{0})\cap\mathbf{F}_{\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})}})\oplus((\mathbf{F}/\mathbf{F}_{0})\cap\mathbf{F}_{-\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})})\oplus(\overline{(\mathbf{F}/\mathbf{F}_{0})\cap\mathbf{F}_{-\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})}}).

Then let
\tilde{I}(\mathbf{F})(\mathbf{w}+\overline{\mathbf{w}}+\mathbf{w}^{\prime}+\overline{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}):=\mathbf{w}+\overline{\mathbf{w}}-\mathbf{w}^{\prime}-\overline{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}
for
\mathbf{w}\in(\mathbf{F}/\mathbf{F}_{0})\cap\mathbf{F}_{\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})})
,
\overline{\mathbf{w}}\in\overline{(\mathbf{F}/\mathbf{F}_{0})\cap\mathbf{F}_{\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})})}
,
\mathbf{w}^{\prime}\in(\mathbf{F}/\mathbf{F}_{0})\cap\mathbf{F}_{-\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})})
,
\overline{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\in\overline{(\mathbf{F}/\mathbf{F}_{0})\cap\mathbf{F}_{-\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})})}
. The rest of the proof can be verified similarly as above, considering additionally the fact that
\tilde{I}(\mathbf{F})
commutes with complex conjugation.
(3): We can proceed similarly to (2), from the splitting of
(W_{0}^{\omega}/W_{0})^{\mathbb{C}}
into
\tilde{\omega}^{\mathbb{C}}(\cdot,\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})\overline{\cdot})
-orthogonal subspaces

(\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}_{\geq 0}/\mathbf{F}_{0})\oplus(\overline{\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}_{\geq 0}/\mathbf{F}_{0}})\oplus(\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}_{\leq 0}/\mathbf{F}_{0})\oplus(\overline{\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}_{\leq 0}/\mathbf{F}_{0}}).

∎

So when an
\omega
-compatible linear complex structure on
V
is given, a choice of compatible triple is determined by a choice of basepoint.

We will denote any choice of
W^{\prime}\in(\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega})|_{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})}^{-1}(W_{0})
,
\mathbf{F}\in(\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{0})|_{\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}^{-1}(W_{0})
, and
\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}\in\alpha|_{\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}(\vec{n})}^{-1}(W_{0})
by
b=b_{\mathbf{J},W_{0}}
, so we can express how the choice of involution depends on other choices as

\tilde{I}=\tilde{I}(b)=\tilde{I}(b,\mathbf{J},W_{0}).

Before we provide the proof of the main theorem, we briefly recall how different objects depend on others. The objects that depend only on
\mathbf{J}
are
K
,
\mathfrak{k}
, and
\mathfrak{p}
, where
K=G(\mathbf{J})=G^{\operatorname{Ad}(\mathbf{J})}\leq G=\operatorname{Sp}(V)
,
and
\operatorname{Lie}(G)=\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{k}\oplus\mathfrak{p}
. The objects that depend only on the choice of an isotropic subspace
W=W_{0}\subseteqq V
are
\tilde{G}=\operatorname{Sp}(W_{0}^{\omega}/W_{0})
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}=\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{G})
. The objects that depend on the choice of both
\mathbf{J}
and
W=W_{0}
are
\tilde{K}=\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0}))
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{k}}=\tilde{\mathfrak{k}}(\mathbf{J},W_{0})
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}=\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbf{J},W_{0})
, and
b=b_{\mathbf{J},W_{0}}
. The objects that depend on the choice of
b
,
\mathbf{J}
, and
W_{0}
are
\tilde{I}=\tilde{I}(b)=\tilde{I}(b,\mathbf{J},W_{0})
,

\tilde{G}^{\shortparallel}=\tilde{G}(\tilde{I})=\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}(b,\mathbf{J},W_{0})),\quad\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}\tilde{\mathbf{J}})=\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}(b)\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})),\quad\tilde{K}^{\shortparallel}=\tilde{G}(\tilde{I}(b),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})),

\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{\shortparallel}&=&\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{\shortparallel}(\tilde{I}(b,\mathbf{J},W_{0}))),&\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{\times}&=&\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{\times}(\tilde{I}(b,\mathbf{J},W_{0})),\\
\tilde{\mathfrak{k}}^{\shortparallel}&=&\tilde{\mathfrak{k}}^{\shortparallel}(\tilde{I}(b),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})),&\tilde{\mathfrak{k}}^{\times}&=&\tilde{\mathfrak{k}}^{\times}(\tilde{I}(b),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})),\\
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}&=&\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}(\tilde{I}(b),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})),&\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}&=&\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}(\tilde{I}(b),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0})).\end{array}

Let
K^{\shortparallel}(b_{\ast}):=K\cap\operatorname{Sp}_{b_{\ast}}(V)
, for
b_{\ast}=\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}\in\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
,
b_{\ast}=\mathbf{F}\in\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
, or
b_{\ast}=W^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})
.

K^{\shortparallel}(b_{\ast})\cong O(n_{0})\times\operatorname{U}(n_{+})\times\operatorname{U}(n_{-})

for any choice of
b_{\ast}
.

Now consider

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}:\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})

\displaystyle W^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I}(W_{\ast}^{\prime}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}((W^{\prime})_{0}),W_{\ast}^{\prime})}(W^{\prime})

where
W_{\ast}^{\prime}
is any point in
(\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega})|_{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})}^{-1}((W^{\prime})_{0})
. By Lemma
4.1
.3,
\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}
only depends on
\mathbf{J}
, and not on the choice of auxiliary basepoints
W_{\ast}^{\prime}
’s used to define it.

Similarly, let

\displaystyle\beta_{\mathbf{J}}:\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\prime}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(\operatorname{Re}\mathbf{F}_{0}),\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\prime})}(\mathbf{F}^{\prime})

where
\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\prime}\in(\operatorname{Re}\cdot 0)|_{\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}^{-1}(\operatorname{Re}\mathbf{F}^{\prime}_{0})
, and let

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathbf{J}}:\operatorname{Lag}_{\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})

\displaystyle(\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I}((\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})_{\ast}^{\prime}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(\operatorname{Re}\mathbf{F}^{\prime}_{0}),(\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})_{\ast}^{\prime})}((\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})^{\prime})

where
(\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})_{\ast}^{\prime}\in(\alpha|_{\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})})^{-1}(\alpha((\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})^{\prime})
.

Note that the existence of the splitting
V=W_{0}\oplus\mathbf{J}W_{0}\oplus W_{-}^{\mathbf{J}}
simultaneously for every isotropic
W_{0}
provides a preferred choice of identifications

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(W_{0}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sp}(W_{0}^{\omega}/W_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sp}_{W_{0}}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sp}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(kW_{0}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sp}(kW_{0}^{\omega}/kW_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sp}_{kW_{0}}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sp}(V)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{J}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ad}(k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ad}(k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{J}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ad}(k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ad}(k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{J}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{J}}

(67)

for any
k\in K
. We have labelled with
\mathbf{J}
, maps that depend on the choice of
\mathbf{J}
. The three vertical arrows on the left are isomorphisms and the rightmost vertical arrow on the right is an automorphism.

Similarly, if
b_{\ast}=\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}\in\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
,
b_{\ast}=\mathbf{F}\in\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
, or
b_{\ast}=W^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})
,
\mathbf{J}
allows us to identify
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
as subspaces of
\mathfrak{g}
, and
\operatorname{Ad}(k)
induces isomorphisms

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\tilde{K}(\operatorname{Re}(b_{\ast})_{0})&\xrightarrow{\cong}&\tilde{K}(k\operatorname{Re}(b_{\ast})_{0}),\\
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(\operatorname{Re}(b_{\ast})_{0})&\xrightarrow{\cong}&\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(k\operatorname{Re}(b_{\ast})_{0}),\\
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}(\tilde{I}(b_{\ast}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}((b_{\ast})_{0}))&\xrightarrow{\cong}&\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}(\tilde{I}(kb_{\ast}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(kb_{\ast})_{0})),\\
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}(\tilde{I}(b_{\ast}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}((b_{\ast})_{0}))&\xrightarrow{\cong}&\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}(\tilde{I}(kb_{\ast}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}((kb_{\ast})_{0})).\end{array}

If
k\in K^{\shortparallel}
, these isomorphisms are automorphisms, and
K^{\shortparallel}
acts on
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(\operatorname{Re}(b_{\ast})_{0})
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}(\tilde{I}(b_{\ast}))
, and
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}(\tilde{I}(b_{\ast}))
where
\operatorname{GL}(\operatorname{Re}(b_{\ast})_{0})\leq K^{\shortparallel}
acts trivially.

Now we are ready to reiterate the proof of Lemma
4.1
.

Theorem 4.5
.

Suppose
\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}\in\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
,
\mathbf{F}_{\ast}\in\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
, and
W_{\ast}\in\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})
.

1.

\eta_{\mathbf{J}}
,
\beta_{\mathbf{J}}
, and
\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}
are
K
-equivariant.

2.

There are diffeomorphisms

\displaystyle K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})}\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(\operatorname{Re}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast})_{0})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\operatorname{Lag}_{\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})

\displaystyle{[(k,\tilde{p}_{\ast})]}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle k\exp\tilde{p}_{\ast}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}

\displaystyle K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast})}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(\operatorname{Re}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast})_{0})/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}(\tilde{I}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(\operatorname{Re}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast})_{0}))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})

\displaystyle{[(k,\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\shortparallel}+\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times})]}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle k\exp\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\shortparallel}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}

\displaystyle K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(W_{\ast})}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}((W_{\ast})_{0})/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}(\tilde{I}(W_{\ast}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}((W_{\ast})_{0}))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})

\displaystyle{[(k,\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\times}+\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel})]}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle k\exp\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\times}.W_{\ast}.

3.

The diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})}\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lag}_{\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K/K^{\shortparallel}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathbf{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(68)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast})}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times})(\mathbf{F}_{\ast})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K/K^{\shortparallel}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{\mathbf{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(69)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(W_{\ast})}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel})(W_{\ast})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K/K^{\shortparallel}(W_{\ast})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(70)

commute. The upper horizontal arrows are defined from the previous statement.

Proof.

(1): Suppose
W^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})
and
W_{\ast}^{\prime}\in(\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega})|_{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})}^{-1}((W^{\prime})_{0})
. By Lemma
4.1
, there exist
\tilde{k}\in\tilde{K}(\mathbf{J},(W^{\prime})_{0})
and
\tilde{p}^{\times}\in\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}(\tilde{I}(W_{\ast}^{\prime}))
such that
W^{\prime}=\tilde{k}e^{\tilde{p}^{\times}}.W_{\ast}^{\prime}
. Then for all
k\in K

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}(k.W^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I}(k.W_{\ast}^{\prime}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(k.(W^{\prime})_{0}),k.W_{\ast}^{\prime})}(k.W^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I}(k.W_{\ast}^{\prime}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(k.(W^{\prime})_{0}),k.W_{\ast}^{\prime})}(k\tilde{k}e^{\tilde{p}^{\times}}.W_{\ast}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{(\tilde{I}(k.W_{\ast}^{\prime}),\tilde{\mathbf{J}}(k.(W^{\prime})_{0}),k.W_{\ast}^{\prime})}(k\tilde{k}k^{-1}\cdot ke^{\tilde{p}^{\times}}k^{-1}.kW_{\ast}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k\tilde{k}k^{-1}.kW_{\ast}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k.\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}(W^{\prime}),

because
\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}(W^{\prime})=\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}(\tilde{k}e^{\tilde{p}^{\times}}.W_{\ast}^{\prime})=\tilde{k}.W_{\ast}^{\prime}
.
So
\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}
is
K
-equivariant. Similarly,
\eta_{\mathbf{J}}
,
\beta_{\mathbf{J}}
are
K
-equivariant. (2): Suppose
(\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
and
(\mathbf{F}^{\oplus}_{\ast})^{\prime}\in\alpha|_{\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}^{-1}(\alpha((\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})^{\prime}))
. By Lemma
4.1
.1, there exist
\tilde{k}\in\tilde{K}(\operatorname{Re}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})^{\prime}_{0})
and
\tilde{p}\in\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(\operatorname{Re}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})^{\prime}_{\geq 0})
such that
(\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})^{\prime}=\tilde{k}e^{\tilde{p}}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})^{\prime}
. Since
K
acts transitively on
\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
, there exists a
k\in K
such that

(\mathbf{F}^{\oplus})^{\prime}=\tilde{k}e^{\tilde{p}}k.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}=\tilde{k}k.e^{\operatorname{Ad}(k)^{-1}\tilde{p}}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}.

So the map
K\times\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(\operatorname{Re}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})_{\geq 0})\to\operatorname{Lag}_{\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
,
(k,\tilde{p}_{\ast})\mapsto ke^{\tilde{p}_{\ast}}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}
is surjective.

If
ke^{\tilde{p}_{\ast}}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}=k^{\prime}e^{\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\prime}}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}
, taking
\eta_{\mathbf{J}}
on both sides, we obtain
k^{-1}k^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Sp}_{\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}}(V)
. So there exists a
k^{\shortparallel}\in K^{\shortparallel}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})
such that
k^{\prime}=kk^{\shortparallel}
. Putting this back in, we get

e^{\tilde{p}_{\ast}}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}=k^{-1}k^{\prime}e^{\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\prime}}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}=e^{\operatorname{Ad}(k^{\shortparallel})^{-1}\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\prime}}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}.

By Lemma
4.1
.1,
\tilde{p_{\ast}}=\operatorname{Ad}(k^{\shortparallel})^{-1}\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\prime}
. So the map
K\times\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(\operatorname{Re}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})_{\geq 0})\to\operatorname{Lag}_{\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
descends to a diffeomorphism
K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})}\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(\operatorname{Re}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})_{\geq 0})\to\operatorname{Lag}_{\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
. Similarly, if
W^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})
, choose
W_{\ast}^{\prime}\in(\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega}|_{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})})^{-1}(W_{0}^{\prime})
. By Lemma
4.1
.1, there exist
\tilde{k}\in\tilde{K}(W_{0}^{\prime})
and
\tilde{p}=\tilde{p}^{\times}+\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}\in\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(W_{0}^{\prime})
such that
W^{\prime}=\tilde{k}e^{\tilde{p}^{\times}}e^{\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}}.W_{\ast}^{\prime}=\tilde{k}e^{\tilde{p}^{\times}}.W_{\ast}^{\prime}
. Since
K
acts transitively on
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})
, there exists a
k\in K
such that
W_{\ast}^{\prime}=k.W_{\ast}
. So

W^{\prime}=\tilde{k}e^{\tilde{p}^{\times}}k.W_{\ast}^{\prime}=\tilde{k}k.e^{\operatorname{Ad}(k)^{-1}\tilde{p}^{\times}}.W_{\ast}.

So map
K\times\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}((W_{\ast}))_{0}\to\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})
,
(\tilde{k},\tilde{p}_{\ast})\mapsto ke^{\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\times}}e^{\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\shortparallel}}.W_{\ast}
is surjective. Proceeding as before, it descends to a diffeomorphism
K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(W_{\ast})}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel})(W_{\ast})\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})
. Similarly, we obtain the diffeomorphism for
K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast})}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel})(\mathbf{F}_{\ast})\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})
. (3): This follows from the previous statement and the construction of
\eta_{\mathbf{J}}
,
\beta_{\mathbf{J}}
, and
\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}
.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

For
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n};V)
compare with Theorem 2 of
[
Tak68
]
in the
n_{0}=0
case, and the holomorphic version in Theorem 11.8.4 of
[
Wol69
]
and the third statement of the Orbit fibration theorem of
[
Wol72
]
.

These maps fit into the commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\oplus}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\oplus,\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Gr}(n_{0},0,n-n_{0})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{\mathbf{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathbf{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{\geq 0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathbf{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{0}|_{\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{\geq 0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha|_{\operatorname{Lag}_{\oplus,\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\cap\cdot^{\omega}|_{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})}}

(71)

where every arrow is
K
-equivariant.

Since Theorem
4.5
shows
\eta_{\mathbf{J}}
,
\beta_{\mathbf{J}}
, and
\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}
are realized as fiber bundles with contractible fibers, we can conclude the following from
[
Dol63
]
:

Corollary 4.7
.

For any
\vec{n}
,
\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})
,
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
, and
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\oplus}(\vec{n})
are homotopy equivalent to the compact left coset space
U(n)/(O(n_{0})\times U(n_{+})\times U(n_{-}))
.

Remark 4.8

(Oriented Grassmannians)
.

From the long exact sequence of homotopy groups, we can compute

\pi_{1}(\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n}))\cong\begin{cases}\{1\}&\text{ if }n_{0}=0\\
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}&\text{ if }0<n_{0}<n\\
\mathbb{Z}&\text{ if }n_{0}=n.\end{cases}

(72)

So if
n_{0}=0
, the oriented Grassmannian
\widetilde{Gr}(\vec{n})
is a disconnected double cover of
\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})
. If
n_{0}>0
, take a
W\in\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})
, and a Darboux basis

\{\mathbf{e}^{0}_{1},\mathbf{e}^{0}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{e}^{0}_{n_{0}},\mathbf{e}^{+},\mathbf{e}^{-},\mathbf{f}^{0}_{1},\mathbf{f}^{0}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{f}^{0}_{n_{0}},\mathbf{f}^{+},\mathbf{f}^{-}\}

associated to
W
by Theorem
2.2
. It provides an orientation on
W
, and the Darboux basis

\{-\mathbf{e}^{0}_{1},\mathbf{e}^{0}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{e}^{0}_{n_{0}},\mathbf{e}^{+},\mathbf{e}^{-},-\mathbf{f}^{0}_{1},\mathbf{f}^{0}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{f}^{0}_{n_{0}},\mathbf{f}^{+},\mathbf{f}^{-}\}

provides an opposite orientation on
W
. So
\operatorname{Sp}(V)
acts transitively on
\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}(\vec{n})
and
\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}(\vec{n})
is a connected double cover of
\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})
.

We observe from (
58
) and Theorem
4.5
that

\begin{array}[]{ccccccl}\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}^{-1}(W^{\prime})&\cong&\varphi^{-1}(\mathbf{F})&\cong&\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}&&W^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n}),\mathbf{F}\in\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})\\
\beta_{\mathbf{J}}^{-1}(\mathbf{F})&\cong&\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{\geq 0}^{-1}(W^{\prime})&\cong&\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}&&W^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n}),\mathbf{F}\in\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n}).\end{array}

The invariant complex structures on the fibers of
\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}
,
\beta_{\mathbf{J}}
,
\eta_{\mathbf{J}}
can be obtained by the same argument as Remark
3.4
and the end of Section
3.4
. Since
\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{ad}(\tilde{\mathbf{J}})
is a linear complex structure on
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel}
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}
(
36
) it extends to an invariant complex structure on the fibers by Proposition IV.3.4 of
[
Hel01
]

We observe from the proof of Theorem
4.5
there is a correction term, depending on the choice of basepoint
\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}\in\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
, and arising from the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff noncommutativity of the exponentials:

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\operatorname{Lag}_{\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})&\xrightarrow{\cong}&K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})}\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(\operatorname{Re}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})_{0})&\xrightarrow{\cong}&\operatorname{Lag}_{\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})\\
ke^{\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}_{\ast}}e^{\tilde{p}^{\times}_{\ast}}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}&\mapsto&[(k,\tilde{p}_{\ast})]&\mapsto&ke^{\tilde{p}^{\times}_{\ast}}e^{\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}_{\ast}}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}\end{array}

(73)

We will let
\chi_{\mathbf{J},\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}}(ke^{\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}_{\ast}}e^{\tilde{p}^{\times}_{\ast}}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}):=ke^{\tilde{p}^{\times}_{\ast}}e^{\tilde{p}^{\shortparallel}_{\ast}}.\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}
.

\chi_{\mathbf{J},\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}}
is the identity map when
n_{+}n_{-}=0
or when restricted to
\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
.

Corollary 4.9
.

The commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lag}_{\oplus}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf{J}}(\vec{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lag}_{\mathbf{J},\oplus}(\vec{n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Re}\cdot_{\geq 0}\circ\chi_{\mathbf{J},\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{\mathbf{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathbf{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\mathbf{J}}}

(74)

is a pullback diagram.

Proof.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})}\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(\varphi(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}))}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times})(\varphi(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\times_{K^{\shortparallel}(\operatorname{Re}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})_{\geq 0})}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}/\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel})(\operatorname{Re}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})_{\geq 0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K/K^{\shortparallel}(\mathbf{F}_{\ast}^{\oplus})}

is a pullback diagram. This can be read off from the elementwise description of the maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[(k,\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\shortparallel}+\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\times})]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[(k,\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\shortparallel}+\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times})]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[(k,\tilde{p}_{\ast}^{\times}+\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\shortparallel})]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[k]}.}

The pullback diagram is identifed with the given diagram with the diffeomorphisms in Theorem
4.5
and (
73
).
∎

Appendix A
Appendix

A.1
Darboux bases and Vectorial Cartan subalgebras

Proposition A.1
.

Suppose
(V,\omega)
is a real, finite dimensional symplectic vector space,
G=\operatorname{Sp}(V)
is the group of linear symplectomorphisms of
V
, and
\mathfrak{g}
is the Lie algebra of
G
. Given a Darboux basis
\{\mathbf{e}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{e}_{n},\mathbf{f}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{f}_{n}\}
of
V
there exists a unique Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{a}_{\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}}
(
vectorial
Cartan subalgebra in the sense of
[
Sug59
]
) of
\mathfrak{g}\subseteq\operatorname{End}(V)
such that the weight space decomposition

V\cong\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathfrak{a}_{\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}}^{\vee}}V_{\alpha}

agrees with

V\cong\mathbb{R}\mathbf{e}_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{R}\mathbf{e}_{n}\oplus\mathbb{R}\mathbf{f}_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{R}\mathbf{f}_{n}.

Conversely, given a Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{a}
of
\mathfrak{g}
contained in
\mathfrak{p}
for some Cartan decomposition
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{k}\oplus\mathfrak{p}
of
\mathfrak{g}
, there exists a Darboux basis
\{\mathbf{e}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{e}_{n},\mathbf{f}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{f}_{n}\}
of
V
such that the weight space decomposition

V\cong\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathfrak{a}^{\vee}}V_{\alpha}

agrees with

V\cong\mathbb{R}\mathbf{e}_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{R}\mathbf{e}_{n}\oplus\mathbb{R}\mathbf{f}_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{R}\mathbf{f}_{n}.

This Darboux basis is unique up to the choice and labelling of vectors within the weight spaces.

Proof.

Suppose we are given a Darboux basis
\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}
of
V
. The linear extension of the map assigning
\mathbf{e}_{j}\mapsto\mathbf{f}_{j}
,
\mathbf{f}_{\mathbf{j}}\mapsto-\mathbf{e}_{j}
for all
1\leq j\leq n
is an
\omega
-compatible linear complex structure
\mathbf{J}
on
V
. It defines an inner product
\omega(\cdot,\mathbf{J}\cdot)
on
V
, an involution
\cdot^{t}
on
\operatorname{End}(V)
by
\omega(g\cdot,\mathbf{J}\cdot)=\omega(\cdot,\mathbf{J}g^{t}\cdot)
, and a Cartan decomposition of
\mathfrak{g}\subseteq\operatorname{End}(V)
by
\mathfrak{k}=\mathfrak{g}\cap\operatorname{End}(V)^{-t}
and
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{g}\cap\operatorname{End}{V}^{t}
.

Let
\{\mathbf{e}^{t},\mathbf{f}^{t}\}
be the dual basis of
\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}
in
V^{\vee}:=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{R}}(V;\mathbb{R})
, and consider the abelian Lie subalgebra

\mathfrak{a}_{\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}}:=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{\mathbf{e}_{j}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{t}-\mathbf{f}_{j}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbf{f}_{j}^{t}:1\leq j\leq n\}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}.

\mathfrak{a}_{\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}}
is a linear subspace of
\mathfrak{p}
and is identified with the diagonal matrices in the image of
\mathfrak{g}
in
\operatorname{Mat}_{2n\times 2n}(\mathbb{R})
. Computing commutators in
\operatorname{Mat}_{2n\times 2n}(\mathbb{R})
, we can verify
\mathfrak{a}_{\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}}
is a Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
.

The weights
\{\alpha_{j}:1\leq j\leq n\}
defined by

\alpha_{j}(\mathbf{e}_{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{t}-\mathbf{f}_{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbf{f}_{k}^{t}):=\begin{cases}1\text{ if }j=k\\
0\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

span
\mathfrak{a}_{\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}}^{\vee}
, and the weight space of
\alpha_{j}
is
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{e}_{j}
and the weight space of
-\alpha_{j}
is
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{f}_{j}
for all
1\leq j\leq n
.

From Corollary 2, Section 1 of
[
Sug59
]
, any two Cartan decompositions are conjugate under the action of
\operatorname{Sp}(V)
, and any two maximal abelian subalgebras
\mathfrak{a}
of
\mathfrak{g}
contained in a
\mathfrak{p}
for some Cartan decomposition
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{k}\oplus\mathfrak{p}
are conjugate under the action of the maximal compact subgroup of
\operatorname{Sp}(V)
corresponding to the Cartan decomposition
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{k}\oplus\mathfrak{p}
.

So for Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{g}
contained in some
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}
for some Cartan decomposition
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}
, there exists a
g\in\operatorname{Sp}(V)
such that
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}=g\mathfrak{a}_{\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}}g^{-1}
. The weight space decomposition of
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}=g\mathfrak{a}_{\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}}g^{-1}
is

V=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathfrak{a}_{\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}}^{\vee}}gV_{\alpha}.

Therefore, if
g
stabilizes the decomposition

V=\mathbb{R}\mathbf{e}_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{R}\mathbf{e}_{n}\oplus\mathbb{R}\mathbf{f}_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{R}\mathbf{f}_{n},

\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}=g\mathfrak{a}_{\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}}g^{-1}=\mathfrak{a}_{\{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}\}}
. So
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
is uniquely determined by the weight space decomposition. This argument also shows that a choice of Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}
contained in
\mathfrak{p}
for some Cartan decomposition uniquely determines a weight space decomposition of
V
into real one-dimensional subspaces. Then the choice of Darboux basis is determined up to the choice of vectors within each weight space, and the choices differ only by rescaling and relabelling of the vectors.
∎

A.2
The generalized Möbius action

In this section we fix a Darboux basis of
V
and assume the identification
V\cong\mathbb{R}^{2n}
,
V^{\mathbb{C}}\cong\mathbb{C}^{2n}
.

The symplectic form takes the form

\omega^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})=\mathbf{w}^{t}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1_{n}\\
1_{n}&0\end{pmatrix}\mathbf{v}\quad\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\in V^{\mathbb{C}}.

We will denote the condition
\mathbf{F}=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\mathbf{v}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{v}_{n}\}

as

\mathbf{F}=\left[\begin{pmatrix}|&&|\\
\mathbf{v}_{1}&\cdots&\mathbf{v}_{n}\\
|&&|\end{pmatrix}\right]=\left[\begin{pmatrix}Q\\
P\end{pmatrix}\right]\quad Q,P\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{C})

(75)

\mathbf{F}
is a complex Lagrangian subspace if and only if it is
n
dimensional and
Q^{t}P=P^{t}Q
, and is of type
\vec{n}
if and only if, in addition, the
n\times n
hermitian matrix
-i(\overline{P}^{t}Q-\overline{Q}^{t}P)
has signature
\vec{n}
.

If
X
is an invertible
n\times n
complex matrix, then

\left[\begin{pmatrix}Q\\
P\end{pmatrix}X\right]=\left[\begin{pmatrix}Q\\
P\end{pmatrix}\right].

Example

(The complex projective line)
.

When
n=1
, every complex one dimensional subspace of
\mathbb{C}^{2}
is complex Lagrangian so
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
can be identified with the complex projective line
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})
via

\left[\begin{pmatrix}q\\
p\end{pmatrix}\right]\mapsto[q:p].

\operatorname{Sp}(2;\mathbb{C})=\operatorname{SL}(2;\mathbb{C})
and
\operatorname{Sp}(2;\mathbb{R})=\operatorname{SL}(2;\mathbb{R})
, so the generalized Möbius action is then the honest Möbius action given by linear fractional transformations.

\kappa|_{[q:p]}=\begin{pmatrix}\overline{q}&\overline{p}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}q\\
p\end{pmatrix}=2\operatorname{Im}(q\overline{p})

So
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(0,1,0)
,
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(1,0,0)
,
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(0,0,1)
can, respectively, be identified with the upper hemisphere, equator, and lower hemisphere when the north and south poles are identified with
[\pm i:1]
.

Example

(Siegel upper half plane)
.

The
Siegel upper half plane
is given by

\{Z\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{C})^{t}:\operatorname{Im}Z>0\},

and can be identified with
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(0,n,0)
by

Z\mapsto\left[\begin{pmatrix}Z\\
1_{n}\end{pmatrix}\right].

The generalized Möbius action of
\operatorname{Sp}(2n;\mathbb{R})
on the Siegel upper half plane defined by

\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}.Z:=(AZ+B)(CZ+D)^{-1}

is then just a consequence of

\left[\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}Z\\
1_{n}\end{pmatrix}\right]=\left[\begin{pmatrix}AZ+B\\
CZ+D\end{pmatrix}\right]=\left[\begin{pmatrix}(AZ+B)(CZ+D)^{-1}\\
1_{n}\end{pmatrix}\right].

A.3
The Harish-Chandra embedding in matrix form

In this section we fix a Darboux basis of
V
and assume the identification
V\cong\mathbb{R}^{2n}
,
V^{\mathbb{C}}\cong\mathbb{C}^{2n}
. We choose an
\omega
-compatible complex structure

\mathbf{J}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1_{n}\\
1_{n}&0\end{pmatrix}.

Then the subgroups defined in Section
3.1
are

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{x}&-\mathbf{y}\\
\mathbf{y}&\mathbf{x}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{x}+i\mathbf{y}\in U(n)\right\}

\displaystyle K^{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{x}&-\mathbf{y}\\
\mathbf{y}&\mathbf{x}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{x}^{t}\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}^{t}\mathbf{y}=1_{n},\mathbf{x}^{t}\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{y}^{t}\mathbf{x}\right\}

\displaystyle G_{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{x}&-\overline{\mathbf{y}}\\
\mathbf{y}&\overline{\mathbf{x}}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{x}+i\mathbf{y}\in U(n)\right\}.

Moreover,

\mathbf{F}_{\pm\mathbf{J}}=\left[\begin{pmatrix}\pm i1_{n}\\
1_{n}\end{pmatrix}\right]

and

\displaystyle P^{+}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}1_{n}-\mathbf{z}&i\mathbf{z}\\
i\mathbf{z}&1_{n}+\mathbf{z}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{z}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{C})^{t}\right\}

\displaystyle P^{-}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}1_{n}+\mathbf{z}&i\mathbf{z}\\
i\mathbf{z}&1_{n}-\mathbf{z}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{z}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{C})^{t}\right\}.

The Harish-Chandra embedding is defined by taking a
gK\in G/K
to the unique element
\mathbf{p}^{+}
of the Lie algebra of
P^{+}
such that

gK^{\mathbb{C}}P^{-}=\exp(\mathbf{p}^{+})K^{\mathbb{C}}P^{-}\in G^{\mathbb{C}}/K^{\mathbb{C}}P^{-}.

Composing the Harish-Chandra embedding with the map
\mathbf{p}^{+}\mapsto\exp(\mathbf{p}^{+})K^{\mathbb{C}}P^{-}
gives the Borel embedding.

In our case, the matrix form the Harish-Chandra embedding can be expressed as

\begin{pmatrix}1_{n}&\mathbf{x}\\
0&1_{n}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{y}&0\\
0&\mathbf{y}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}K\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}-\mathbf{z}&i\mathbf{z}\\
i\mathbf{z}&\mathbf{z}\end{pmatrix}

where
\mathbf{x}
and
\mathbf{y}
are real symmetric
n\times n
matrices,
\mathbf{y}
is strictly positive definite, and

\mathbf{z}=\frac{1}{2}(1_{n}+i(\mathbf{x}-i\mathbf{y}^{2}))(1_{n}-i(\mathbf{x}-i\mathbf{y}^{2}))^{-1}.

(76)

(c.f. the Cayley transform
\mathbf{z}\mapsto(1_{n}+i\mathbf{z})(1_{n}-i\mathbf{z})^{-1}
mapping the Siegel upper half plane to the Siegel disk consisting of all
n\times n
complex symmetric matrices
\mathbf{z}
such that
1_{n}-\overline{\mathbf{z}}\mathbf{z}
is positive definite.)

(
76
) can be computed by equating the coefficients

\begin{pmatrix}1_{n}&\mathbf{x}\\
0&1_{n}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{y}&0\\
0&\mathbf{y}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\left[\begin{pmatrix}-i1_{n}\\
1_{n}\end{pmatrix}\right]=\left[\begin{pmatrix}-i(\mathbf{y}^{2}+i\mathbf{x})\\
1_{n}\end{pmatrix}\right]

\begin{pmatrix}1_{n}-\mathbf{z}&i\mathbf{z}\\
i\mathbf{z}&1+\mathbf{z}\end{pmatrix}\left[\begin{pmatrix}-i1_{n}\\
1_{n}\end{pmatrix}\right]=\left[\begin{pmatrix}-i(1-2\mathbf{z})(1+2\mathbf{z})^{-1}\\
1_{n}\end{pmatrix}\right].

In particular, the image of the Harish-Chandra embedding is a bounded domain biholomorphic to

\{\mathbf{z}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{C})^{t}:4\cdot 1_{n}-\mathbf{z}\mathbf{z}^{\dagger}>0\}

so the closure of
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(0,0,n)
in
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
is homeomorphic to a closed
n(n+1)
-ball.

A.4
Matrix description of Lie Algebra

In this section we fix a Darboux basis of
V
and assume the identification
V\cong\mathbb{R}^{2n}
,
V^{\mathbb{C}}\cong\mathbb{C}^{2n}
.

A.4.1
Cartan decomposition and linear complex structure

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be the lie algebra of
\operatorname{Sp}(2n;\mathbb{R})
. The Cartan decomposition
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{k}\oplus\mathfrak{p}
with respect to the involution
\theta(\cdot):=-\cdot^{t}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{a}&\mathbf{b}\\
\mathbf{c}&-\mathbf{a}^{t}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{b},\mathbf{c}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{R})^{t},\mathbf{a}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{R})\right\}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{a}&-\mathbf{b}\\
\mathbf{b}&\mathbf{a}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{a}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{R})^{-t},\mathbf{b}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{R})^{t}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}\cap\operatorname{Mat}_{2n\times 2n}(\mathbb{R})^{-t}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{a}&\mathbf{b}\\
\mathbf{b}&-\mathbf{a}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{R})^{t}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}\cap\operatorname{Mat}_{2n\times 2n}(\mathbb{R})^{t}.

The element

\mathbf{J}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1_{n}\\
1_{n}&0\end{pmatrix}

(77)

in the center of
\mathfrak{k}
is such that

\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{ad}(\mathbf{J})\left(\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{a}&\mathbf{b}\\
\mathbf{b}&-\mathbf{a}\end{pmatrix}\right)=\mathbf{J}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{a}&\mathbf{b}\\
\mathbf{b}&-\mathbf{a}\end{pmatrix}\right)=\begin{pmatrix}-\mathbf{b}&\mathbf{a}\\
\mathbf{a}&\mathbf{b}\end{pmatrix}.

(78)

So
(\operatorname{ad}(\mathbf{J})|_{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}=-1_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and is a linear complex structure on
\mathfrak{p}
, with
\pm i
-eigenspaces

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}-\mathbf{z}&i\mathbf{z}\\
i\mathbf{z}&\mathbf{z}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{z}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{C})^{t}\right\}\subseteq\mathfrak{p}^{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{-}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{z}&i\mathbf{z}\\
i\mathbf{z}&-\mathbf{z}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{z}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{C})^{t}\right\}\subseteq\mathfrak{p}^{\mathbb{C}}.

Unlike
\mathfrak{p}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{-}
are abelian Lie subalgebras of
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
.

A.4.2
Root space decompositions

Maximal abelian subalgebras
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{h}
of
\mathfrak{g}
contained in
\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathfrak{t}\subseteq\mathfrak{k}
are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{d}&0\\
0&-\mathbf{d}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{d}=\operatorname{diag}(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}),d_{j}\in\mathbb{R},1\leq j\leq n\right\}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathbf{d}\\
\mathbf{d}&0\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{d}=\operatorname{diag}(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}),d_{j}\in\mathbb{R},1\leq j\leq n\right\}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}0&-\mathbf{d}\\
\mathbf{d}&0\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{d}=\operatorname{diag}(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}),d_{j}\in\mathbb{R},1\leq j\leq n\right\}

The weights of the fundamental representation are

\displaystyle\mu_{j}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{d}&0\\
0&-\mathbf{d}\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle d_{j}\quad 1\leq j\leq n

\displaystyle\delta_{j}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathbf{d}\\
\mathbf{d}&0\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle d_{j}\quad 1\leq j\leq n

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{j}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0&-\mathbf{d}\\
\mathbf{d}&0\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle d_{j}\quad 1\leq j\leq n.

The roots are, with uncorrelated choice of signs, elements of

\displaystyle\Phi(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{a})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{\pm\mu_{j}\pm\mu_{\ell}:1\leq j,\ell\leq n\}\setminus\{0\}

\displaystyle\Phi(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{h})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{\pm\delta_{j}\pm\delta_{\ell}:1\leq j,\ell\leq n\}\setminus\{0\}

\displaystyle\Phi(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}};\mathfrak{t}^{\mathbb{C}})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{\pm i\varepsilon_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{\ell}:1\leq j,\ell\leq n\}\setminus\{0\}.

The positive and simple roots are chosen as

\displaystyle\Phi^{+}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{a})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{\mu_{j}-\mu_{\ell}:1\leq j<\ell\leq n\}\sqcup\{\mu_{j}+\mu_{\ell}:1\leq j\leq\ell\leq n\}

\displaystyle\Delta(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{a})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{\mu_{1}-\mu_{2},\cdots,\mu_{n-1}-\mu_{n},2\mu_{n}\}

\displaystyle\Phi^{+}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{h})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{\delta_{j}-\delta_{\ell}:1\leq j<\ell\leq n\}\sqcup\{\delta_{j}+\delta_{\ell}:1\leq j\leq\ell\leq n\}

\displaystyle\Delta(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{h})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{\delta_{1}-\delta_{2},\cdots,\delta_{n-1}-\delta_{n},2\delta_{n}\}

\displaystyle\Phi^{+}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}};\mathfrak{t}^{\mathbb{C}})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{i\varepsilon_{j}-i\varepsilon_{\ell}:1\leq j<\ell\leq n\}\sqcup\{i\varepsilon_{j}+i\varepsilon_{\ell}:1\leq j\leq\ell\leq n\}

\displaystyle\Delta(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}};\mathfrak{t}^{\mathbb{C}})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{i\varepsilon_{1}-i\varepsilon_{2},\cdots,i\varepsilon_{n-1}-i\varepsilon_{n},2i\varepsilon_{n}\}

With respect to this choice, the fundamental representation has highest weights
\mu_{1}
,
\delta_{1}
,
\varepsilon_{1}
.

The Weyl group
\mathbb{S}_{n}\ltimes(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})^{n}
acts on
\Phi(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{a})
by permuting the indices and flipping signs

\displaystyle\sigma.(\pm\mu_{j}\pm\mu_{\ell})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\mu_{\sigma(j)}\pm\mu_{\sigma(\ell)}\quad\sigma\in\Sigma_{n}

\displaystyle(\epsilon_{1},\cdots,\epsilon_{n}).(\pm\mu_{j}\pm\mu_{\ell})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\epsilon_{j}\mu_{j}\pm\epsilon_{\ell}\mu_{\ell}\quad\epsilon_{j}\in\{\pm 1\},1\leq j\leq n

and similarly on
\Phi(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{h})
and
\Phi(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}};\mathfrak{t}^{\mathbb{C}})
.

The
n\times n
matrix with
1
on the entry in the
j
th row and
\ell
th column, and rest of entries zero is denoted as
E_{j\ell}
.

The root spaces for
\Phi(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{a})
are

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\mu_{j}-\mu_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{\begin{pmatrix}E_{j\ell}&0\\
0&-E_{\ell j}\end{pmatrix}\right\}\quad j\neq\ell

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\mu_{j}+\mu_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{\begin{pmatrix}0&E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{-\mu_{j}-\mu_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}&0\end{pmatrix}\right\}

where
1\leq j,\ell\leq n
.

The root spaces for
\Phi(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{h})
are

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\delta_{j}-\delta_{\ell}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{\begin{pmatrix}E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}&E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}\\
E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}&E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}\end{pmatrix}\right\}\quad j\neq\ell

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\delta_{j}+\delta_{\ell}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{\begin{pmatrix}-E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}&E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}\\
-E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}&E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}\end{pmatrix}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{-\delta_{j}-\delta_{\ell}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{\begin{pmatrix}-E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}&-E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}\\
E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}&E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}\end{pmatrix}\right\}

where
1\leq j,\ell\leq n
.

The root spaces for
\Phi(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}};\mathfrak{t}^{\mathbb{C}})
are

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{i\varepsilon_{j}-i\varepsilon_{\ell}}^{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\left\{\begin{pmatrix}E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}&i(E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j})\\
-i(E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j})&E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}\end{pmatrix}\right\}\subseteq\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}\quad j\neq\ell

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{i\varepsilon_{j}+i\varepsilon_{\ell}}^{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\left\{\begin{pmatrix}-E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}&i(E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j})\\
i(E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j})&E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}\end{pmatrix}\right\}\subseteq\mathfrak{p}^{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{-i\varepsilon_{j}-i\varepsilon_{\ell}}^{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\left\{\begin{pmatrix}E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}&i(E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j})\\
i(E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j})&-E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}\end{pmatrix}\right\}\subseteq\mathfrak{p}^{\mathbb{C}}

where
1\leq j,\ell\leq n
.

\Phi(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}};\mathfrak{t}^{\mathbb{C}})
partitions into the compact roots
\{\pm(i\varepsilon_{j}-i\varepsilon_{\ell}):1\leq j<\ell\leq n\}
and the noncompact roots
\{\pm(i\varepsilon_{j}+i\varepsilon_{\ell}):1\leq j,\ell\leq n\}
.

A.4.3
Choice of root vectors

The Killing form on
\mathfrak{g}
is

\left\langle\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{a}&\mathbf{b}\\
\mathbf{c}&-\mathbf{a}^{t}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{a}^{\prime}&\mathbf{b}^{\prime}\\
\mathbf{c}^{\prime}&-(\mathbf{a}^{\prime})^{t}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle=2(n+1)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime}\mathbf{a}+\mathbf{b}\mathbf{c}^{\prime}+\mathbf{c}^{\prime}\mathbf{b}).

The coroots of
\pm i\varepsilon_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{\ell}\in\Phi(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}};\mathfrak{t}^{\mathbb{C}})
are, with correlated choice of signs,

h_{\pm i(\varepsilon_{j}-i\varepsilon_{\ell})}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&\pm iE_{jj}\mp iE_{\ell\ell}\\
\mp iE_{jj}\pm iE_{jj}&0\end{pmatrix}

h_{\pm i(\varepsilon_{j}+i\varepsilon_{\ell})}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&\pm iE_{jj}\pm iE_{\ell\ell}\\
\mp iE_{jj}\mp iE_{jj}&0\end{pmatrix}

Choose root vectors, for
1\leq j,\ell\leq n
,

\displaystyle e_{i\varepsilon_{j}-i\varepsilon_{\ell}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}&i(E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j})\\
-i(E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j})&E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathfrak{g}_{i\varepsilon_{j}-i\varepsilon_{\ell}}^{\mathbb{C}}\quad j\neq\ell

\displaystyle e_{i\varepsilon_{j}+i\varepsilon_{\ell}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}-E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}&i(E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j})\\
i(E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j})&E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathfrak{g}_{i\varepsilon_{j}+i\varepsilon_{\ell}}^{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle e_{-i\varepsilon_{j}-i\varepsilon_{\ell}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}-E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}&-i(E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j})\\
-i(E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j})&E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathfrak{g}_{-i\varepsilon_{j}-i\varepsilon_{\ell}}^{\mathbb{C}}.

Then
[e_{\varphi},e_{-\varphi}]=h_{\varphi}
for all
\varphi\in\Phi(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}};\mathfrak{t}^{\mathbb{C}})
.

For each positive noncompact root
\varphi=i\varepsilon_{j}+i\varepsilon_{\ell}
, let

\displaystyle x_{\varphi}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle e_{\varphi}+e_{-\varphi}=\begin{pmatrix}-E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}&0\\
0&E_{j\ell}+E_{\ell j}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle y_{\varphi}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle i(e_{\varphi}-e_{-\varphi})=\begin{pmatrix}0&-E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}\\
-E_{j\ell}-E_{\ell j}&0\end{pmatrix}.

Then
\{x_{\varphi},y_{\varphi}\}_{\{i\varepsilon_{j}+i\varepsilon_{\ell}:1\leq j,\ell\leq n\}}
is a basis of
\mathfrak{p}
and

\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{ad}(\mathbf{J})x_{\varphi}=y_{\varphi},\quad\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{ad}(\mathbf{J})y_{\varphi}=-x_{\varphi},\quad[x_{\varphi},y_{\varphi}]=-2ih_{\varphi}.

A.4.4
Partial Cayley Transforms

Two roots
\varphi,\psi\in\Phi(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}};\mathfrak{t}^{\mathbb{C}})
are
strongly orthogonal
if neither
\varphi\pm\psi
is a root. We choose a maximal strongly orthogonal collection of positive noncompact roots

\Psi:=\{2i\varepsilon_{1},\cdots,2i\varepsilon_{n}\}.

(79)

We choose

\displaystyle\Gamma

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{2i\varepsilon_{1},\cdots,2i\varepsilon_{n_{0}}\}\subseteq\Psi

(80)

\displaystyle\Sigma

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{2i\varepsilon_{n_{0}+1},\cdots,2i\varepsilon_{n_{0}+n_{+}}\}\subseteq\Psi,

(81)

and suppress denoting the dependence of
n_{0}
,
n_{-}
, because if
|\Gamma|=|\Gamma^{\prime}|
, for another subset
\Gamma^{\prime}\subseteq\Psi
then
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
(respectively
\Sigma
and
\Sigma^{\prime}
) differ by a Weyl group transformation.

The partial Cayley transforms are

c_{\Gamma}:=\prod_{\gamma\in\Gamma}c_{\gamma},\quad c_{2i\varepsilon_{j}}:=\exp\left(\frac{i\pi}{4}y_{2i\varepsilon_{j}}\right).

So

c_{\Gamma}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}1_{n_{0}}&0&0&-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}1_{n_{0}}&0&0\\
0&1_{n_{+}}&0&0&0&0\\
0&0&1_{n_{-}}&0&0&0\\
-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}1_{n_{0}}&0&0&\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}1_{n_{0}}&0&0\\
0&0&0&0&1_{n_{+}}&0\\
0&0&0&0&0&1_{n_{-}}\end{pmatrix}

(82)

c_{\Sigma}=\begin{pmatrix}1_{n_{0}}&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}1_{n_{+}}&0&0&-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}1_{n_{+}}&0\\
0&0&1_{n_{-}}&0&0&0\\
0&0&0&1_{n_{0}}&0&0\\
0&-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}1_{n_{+}}&0&0&\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}1_{n_{+}}&0\\
0&0&0&0&0&1_{n_{-}}\end{pmatrix}.

(83)

A.4.5
Symmetries of holomorphic arc components

The set of all roots orthogonal to
\Psi\setminus\Gamma
is, with uncorrelated signs,

(\Psi\setminus\Gamma)^{\perp}=\{\pm i\varepsilon_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{\ell}:n_{0}<j,\ell\leq n\}\setminus\{0\},

The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{t}^{\mathbb{C}}+\sum_{\varphi\in(\Psi\setminus\Gamma)^{\perp}}\mathfrak{g}_{\varphi}^{\mathbb{C}}
consists of all complex
2n\times 2n
matrices

\begin{pmatrix}0&0&-\mathbf{d}&0\\
0&\mathbf{a}&0&\mathbf{b}\\
\mathbf{d}&0&0&0\\
0&\mathbf{c}&0&-\mathbf{a}\end{pmatrix}

such that

\mathbf{d}=\operatorname{diag}(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n_{0}})\quad d_{j}\in\mathbb{C},1\leq j\leq n_{0}

and

\mathbf{a}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{(n-n_{0})\times(n-n_{0})}(\mathbb{C}),\quad\mathbf{b},\mathbf{c}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{(n-n_{0})\times(n-n_{0})}(\mathbb{C})^{t}.

Its derived algebra is

\mathfrak{g}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\mathbb{C}}=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0&0\\
0&\mathbf{a}&0&\mathbf{b}\\
0&0&0&0\\
0&\mathbf{c}&0&-\mathbf{a}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{b},\mathbf{c}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{(n-n_{0})\times(n-n_{0})}(\mathbb{C})^{t}\right\}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}.

Let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathfrak{g}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}\cap\mathfrak{k}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}\cap\mathfrak{p}

so that
\mathfrak{g}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}=\mathfrak{k}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}\oplus\mathfrak{p}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}
.

We can compute

c_{\Sigma}^{4}=\begin{pmatrix}1_{n_{0}}&0&0&0&0\\
0&-1_{n_{+}}&0&0&0&0\\
0&0&1_{n_{-}}&0&0&0\\
0&0&0&1_{n_{0}}&0&0\\
0&0&0&0&-1_{n_{+}}&0\\
0&0&0&0&0&1_{n_{-}}\end{pmatrix}.

Let, with correlating signs,
\mathfrak{g}^{\pm\Sigma}:=\{v\in\mathfrak{g}:\operatorname{Ad}(c_{\Sigma}^{4})v=\pm v\}
.

Then let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{+\Sigma}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{+\Sigma}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{+\Sigma}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{-\Sigma}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{-\Sigma}.

Then

\mathfrak{g}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}=\mathfrak{k}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}\oplus\mathfrak{p}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}\oplus\mathfrak{q}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}\oplus\mathfrak{r}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&\mathbf{a}_{++}&0&0&-\mathbf{b}_{++}&0\\
0&0&\mathbf{a}_{--}&0&0&-\mathbf{b}_{--}\\
0&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&\mathbf{b}_{++}&0&0&\mathbf{a}_{++}&0\\
0&0&\mathbf{b}_{--}&0&0&\mathbf{a}_{--}\end{pmatrix}:\begin{aligned} \mathbf{a}_{\pm\pm}&\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n_{\pm}\times n_{\pm}}(\mathbb{R})^{-t},\\
\mathbf{b}_{\pm\pm}&\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n_{\pm}\times n_{\pm}}(\mathbb{R})^{t}\end{aligned}\right\}

(84)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&\mathbf{a}_{++}&0&0&\mathbf{b}_{++}&0\\
0&0&\mathbf{a}_{--}&0&0&\mathbf{b}_{--}\\
0&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&\mathbf{b}_{++}&0&0&-\mathbf{a}_{++}&0\\
0&0&\mathbf{b}_{--}&0&0&-\mathbf{a}_{--}\end{pmatrix}:\begin{aligned} \mathbf{a}_{\pm\pm}&\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n_{\pm}\times n_{\pm}}(\mathbb{R})^{t},\\
\mathbf{b}_{\pm\pm}&\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n_{\pm}\times n_{\pm}}(\mathbb{R})^{t}\end{aligned}\right\}

(85)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&0&-\mathbf{a}_{-+}^{t}&0&0&-\mathbf{b}_{-+}^{t}\\
0&\mathbf{a}_{-+}&0&0&-\mathbf{b}_{-+}&0\\
0&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&0&\mathbf{b}_{-+}^{t}&0&0&-\mathbf{a}_{-+}^{t}\\
0&\mathbf{b}_{-+}&0&0&\mathbf{a}_{-+}&0\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{a}_{-+},\mathbf{b}_{-+}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n_{-}\times n_{+}}(\mathbb{R})\right\}

(86)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&0&\mathbf{a}_{-+}^{t}&0&0&\mathbf{b}_{-+}^{t}\\
0&\mathbf{a}_{-+}&0&0&\mathbf{b}_{-+}&0\\
0&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&0&\mathbf{b}_{-+}^{t}&0&0&\mathbf{a}_{-+}^{t}\\
0&\mathbf{b}_{-+}&0&0&\mathbf{a}_{-+}&0\end{pmatrix}:\mathbf{a}_{-+},\mathbf{b}_{-+}\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n_{-}\times n_{+}}(\mathbb{R})\right\}.

(87)

Let
G_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\mathbb{C}}
,
G_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}
,
G_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}
,
K_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}
,
K_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}
, be the Lie groups corresponding to the Lie algebras
\mathfrak{g}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\mathbb{C}}
,
\mathfrak{g}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}
,
\mathfrak{g}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}
,
\mathfrak{k}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}
,
\mathfrak{k}_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}
. Then

\displaystyle G_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sp}(2(n-n_{0});\mathbb{C})

\displaystyle G_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sp}(2(n-n_{0});\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle G_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sp}(2n_{+};\mathbb{R})\times\operatorname{Sp}(2n_{-};\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle K_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle U(n-n_{0})

\displaystyle K_{\Psi\setminus\Gamma}^{\Sigma}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle U(n_{+})\times U(n_{-}).

A.5
Figures

Figure A.1:
The Grassmannians
\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})
are tabulated with vertical coordinate
n_{+}-n_{-}
and horizontal coordinate
n_{0}
. The rows describe the partition of the ordinary Grassmannians by the Grassmannians
\operatorname{Gr}(\vec{n})
. Grassmannians of, respectively, symplectic, isotropic, coisotropic subspaces are located on, respectively, left side, lower right, and upper right side of the triangle. Only the isotropic and coisotropic Grassmannians are compact. Taking symplectic complements identifies orbits symmetric about the horizontal line
n_{+}=n_{-}
.

Figure A.2:
The
G
-orbits
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
are tabulated with vertical position
n_{+}-n_{-}
and horizontal position
n_{0}
. The shaded regions indicate
G
-orbits
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n})
in the closures of
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(0,3,1)
,
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(1,1,2)
(and
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(2,1,1)
). Only
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(4,0,0)
is compact. Complex conjugation identifies the orbits symmetric about the horizontal line
n_{+}=n_{-}
.

Figure A.3:
Example of Corollary
4.7
.
Gr(\vec{n};\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\operatorname{Lag}^{\mathbb{C}}(\vec{n};\mathbb{R}^{2})
are homotopy equivalent.
